Compounding this, `agents/tools.py`'s `execute_sql` tool hardcodes `"approved": True` in its payload to the execution sandbox, so the one place downstream that does check an approval flag (`execution_sandbox_service/main.py`'s `if not job.approved: raise HTTPException(403,...)`) is unconditionally satisfied by the tool itself.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** `aurabackend/agents/tool_registry.py`'s `ToolRegistry.call()` now takes a keyword-only `approved: bool = False` and gates before ever reaching `tool.fn`: when `tool.requires_approval or tool.is_destructive` and `not approved`, it records a `ToolCallRecord(approved=False, output=None)` and raises a new `ToolApprovalRequiredError` (a `PermissionError` subclass) instead of executing — enforced ahead of the `dry_run` branch too, so dry-run can't be used to route around it. Once past the gate, the resolved approval status is forwarded into `tool.fn`'s kwargs via `inspect.signature` (only when the function declares an `approved` parameter, so it's a no-op for the other ~8 registered tools). `agents/tools.py`'s `execute_sql` now takes `approved: bool = True` and forwards it into the sandbox payload instead of a hardcoded literal — no longer a blind constant, though its default still preserves today's behavior since `execute_sql` itself is not flagged `requires_approval`/`is_destructive` (deliberate: flipping that flag would gate all ~12 existing agent call sites that call it unapproved, which is a much larger behavior change than this bug asked for — left as an explicit follow-up decision, not bundled in silently). **Judgment call:** used a simple synchronous reject-with-message (`ToolApprovalRequiredError`) rather than routing through UASR's `PENDING_APPROVAL`/reaper queue (BUG-013's precedent) — that queue is a persistent, timeout-driven, async human-in-the-loop mechanism built for long-running self-healing repairs; `ToolRegistry.call()` is a synchronous in-process call from an agent loop with no natural "wait for a human" point, so a synchronous gate is the better shape here per the task's own fallback guidance. Tests: `aurabackend/tests/test_tool_registry_approval.py` (new) — proves a `requires_approval=True`/`is_destructive=True` tool raises and does not execute (dry-run included), executes once `approved=True` is passed, unflagged tools are unaffected, and the resolved approval status is forwarded to a tool function shaped like `execute_sql`.

## BUG-027: counterfactual engine.py's signing path ignores the admin key-revocation flag
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** codebase-quality-audit workflow, 2026-09-02.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (compliance) — `POST /counterfactual/admin/revoke-key` does not actually stop the revoked key from signing new fair-lending/hiring/insurance audit certificates.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/counterfactual_service/engine.py:1812`'s `run_job()` calls `signing.sign_bytes(...)` unconditionally, with no `cryptography.is_revoked()` check — unlike `financial_report.py:103`, which explicitly documents and implements "refuses to sign if the active key is revoked." `main.py:1101`'s `get_sth` (Merkle-tree-head signing) has the same gap. The revoked flag is only ever read in two places (the JWKS endpoint, and `financial_report.py`) — never in the primary counterfactual-audit product this service exists for.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing; the revocation mechanism itself works, it's just not consulted by these two signing paths.
- **Fix:** mirrored `financial_report.py:103`'s exact contract (`sig = None if cryptography.is_revoked() else signing.sign_bytes(canonical)`) in both places: `aurabackend/counterfactual_service/engine.py`'s `run_job()` (added a `cryptography` import; the existing `if sig_b64 is not None: ... signature_status = "signed" else: signature_status = "unsigned"` branch below it needed no change — it already implements the same "None means unsigned" contract `financial_report.py` and `main.py`'s `STHResponse` use) and `aurabackend/counterfactual_service/main.py`'s `get_sth()` (already imported `cryptography`; `signature_status`/`signature_b64`/`signing_key_source` were already conditioned on `sig_b64` truthiness, so no downstream change needed there either). Tests: `aurabackend/tests/test_counterfactual_sprint9.py::test_run_job_refuses_to_sign_with_a_revoked_key` (new) proves a revoked key makes `run_job()` return `signature_status="unsigned"`/`signature_b64=None`, mirroring `test_financial_audit.py::test_revoked_key_yields_unsigned`'s pattern; `aurabackend/tests/test_counterfactual_sth_revocation.py` (new) proves the same for `get_sth()`, plus a control test that signing still succeeds when the key is not revoked.

## BUG-028: frontend — chat errors collapse to one message; a UASR panel re-fires its fetch on every parent re-render
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** codebase-quality-audit workflow, 2026-09-02.
- **Severity:** cosmetic / degrades-accuracy (not a backend/security issue).
- **Root cause:** two independent frontend bugs:
  1. `frontend/src/workbench/Workbench.tsx:281-293`'s chat handler wraps `chatService.streamMessage(...)` in a bare `catch {}` with no parameter, collapsing every failure mode (expired JWT, 5xx, network abort, JSON parse error) into one hardcoded "Commander offline" message — a user hitting an auth failure is told to "connect a gateway" instead of to re-authenticate.
  2. `frontend/src/terminal/panels/PipelinePanel.tsx:305`'s `UasrRecoveries` component does `if (!loaded) { void load(); }` directly in the render body instead of inside a `useEffect` — since the parent re-renders every ~8s telemetry tick/SSE event and `loaded` only flips once the async fetch resolves, any re-render landing before that resolution re-fires a duplicate `GET /uasr/recovery/pending`. Self-corrects once `loaded` becomes true; not a hang, just redundant requests.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing.
- **Fix:** (1) `Workbench.tsx` — gave the `catch` a parameter and added a `describeChatError` helper that inspects `chatService.streamMessage`'s thrown `Error` (it throws plain `Error`s: `stream failed: ${status}` on a non-ok HTTP response, `commander_disabled` on 404, or whatever `fetch` itself throws for a network failure/abort — no typed status). 401/403 now surfaces "Your session has expired — please sign in again to continue.", 5xx surfaces a "temporarily unavailable" message, `AbortError` surfaces "Request cancelled.", and everything else (network failure, 404/disabled) keeps the original "Commander offline" copy. (2) `PipelinePanel.tsx` — moved `UasrRecoveries`'s lazy load into `useEffect(() => { void load(); }, [load])` (mount-only; `load` is a stable `useCallback([])` reference), matching the fetch-on-mount pattern already used elsewhere in the file; the now-unused `loaded` read was dropped, keeping the state write for the async `finally` but nothing reads it anymore since the render-body guard is gone.
  Tests: `frontend/src/workbench/Workbench.test.tsx` (new `describe('Ask AURA chat error branching (BUG-028)')` block — asserts a 401 produces the re-auth message, a network `TypeError` produces the offline message, and the two differ) and the new `frontend/src/terminal/pipeline/__tests__/PipelinePanel.uasr.test.tsx` (selects the UASR node, forces a parent re-render while `healingService.pending()` is still an unresolved promise, and asserts it was called exactly once before and after resolution).
  Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src --max-warnings 0`, `npx vitest run` (67 files / 311 tests) all clean from `frontend/`.

## BUG-029: lower-priority items from the 2026-09-02 codebase-quality audit (logged, not yet triaged for a fix)
- **Status:** fixed — all 7 items resolved (items 2-3 were deferred, picked back up 2026-09-09 as part of a live-verification pass).
- **Found by:** codebase-quality-audit workflow, 2026-09-02 (7 low-severity findings, not run through the adversarial verify pass — that pass was reserved for medium/high findings; treat the still-open ones as plausible, not confirmed).
- **Severity:** low (cosmetic/duplication/readability) except where noted.
- **Findings:**
  1. **Fixed.** `aurabackend/connectors/main.py:324` — `_connection_store` (meant to map `connection_id` → connector config) is never written anywhere in the codebase; every `/connections/{id}/query` call silently fell through to one global env-configured DB regardless of `connection_id`. Fixed the same way as BUG-010's UNION step: a `connection_id` other than the documented `"default"` sentinel now 404s with a clear "not registered" message instead of silently running against the wrong DB; `"default"` keeps its existing env-fallback behavior unchanged. Test: `aurabackend/tests/test_connectors_query_endpoint_bug029.py` (2/2 pass).
  2. **Fixed 2026-09-09.** `aurabackend/ingestion_service/kafka_client.py:27` — no application-level idempotency/dedup key check before publish; `enable_idempotence=True`'s comment overstates what it actually guarantees (only dedups broker-retries within one send call, not two independent client re-POSTs of the same `batch_id`).
  3. **Fixed 2026-09-09.** `aurabackend/shared/file_service.py:82` — `FileService.save_file`/`process_file` were dead in production (the live `/upload` route uses `shared/storage`+`shared/data_utils.py` instead), untested-by-integration, and would have violated the async-safety rule if ever wired in (`process_file` ran blocking pandas I/O with no `to_thread`).
  4. **Fixed 2026-09-02** (landed opportunistically in PR #298 alongside BUG-019). `aurabackend/evolution/api.py:240` — `feedback_summary()` did `__import__("datetime").datetime.now(...)` instead of just adding `datetime` to the existing `from datetime import timedelta, timezone` two lines above; now `from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone`.
  5. **Fixed.** `frontend/src/pages/PipelinesPanel.tsx:868` — the AI-pipeline-run and visual-builder result blocks were near-duplicate ~70-line JSX structures. Extracted a shared `PipelineResultCard` component (composed from `@/components/ui-kit` per house style) that both call sites now render, passing through the fields that vary (title, error, row counts, stats label, output columns, generated SQL, preview rows, download file/handler). Pure refactor — no behavior change; `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint --max-warnings 0`, and `vitest run` (311/311) all clean.
  6. **Fixed.** `frontend/src/pages/PipelinesPanel.tsx:372` (and 5 other sites) — six unconditional `console.log` calls dumping full pipeline payloads (including AI prompts and `JSON.stringify`'d execution configs) to the browser console in production, not gated behind a dev flag. Removed all six; the three `console.error` calls in the same file were kept (legitimate failure diagnostics, not payload dumps) — `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint --max-warnings 0` both clean.
  7. **Fixed.** `frontend/src/workbench/Workbench.tsx:90` — 873-line single component combining shell chrome, chat, forensic-audit, healing-queue approvals, and the live radar model; each concern is independently extractable but the merge into "one shell" was noted as a deliberate earlier design choice per the file's own comments — a real maintainability cost but not a bug, and a large refactor out of scope for a surgical fix.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing across a long project history.
- **Fix:** all 7 items fixed (see above and below). Item 2: `publish_with_retry` now takes an optional `dedup_key`, checked against a bounded (10k-entry) in-memory recently-published set before every publish — a duplicate `dedup_key` is skipped with a warning log instead of re-publishing; the ledger ingestion call site now passes `dedup_key=payload.batch_id`. The misleading `enable_idempotence=True` comment was corrected to state what it actually covers. Test: `aurabackend/tests/test_ingestion_kafka_client_dedup.py` (4/4 pass — duplicate skip, distinct keys both publish, no-key-never-skips, window eviction). Item 3: `save_file`/`process_file` and their now-orphaned tests were deleted outright rather than patched — they were confirmed to have zero production callers (`grep` for `.save_file(`/`.process_file(` across the repo found only their own unit tests), so removing the risk entirely is simpler and more in line with "don't handle errors that can't happen" than adding `to_thread` wrapping to dead code. `validate_file`, `calculate_file_hash`, `_profile_dataframe`, `_infer_dtype`, and `_to_serializable` were kept — each still has live callers or is directly exercised by `tests/test_integration.py`. 43/43 broader regression clean (`test_file_service.py`, `test_ingestion_resilience.py`, `test_ingestion_security.py`, plus the new dedup test), ruff clean.
  Item 7's split: `Workbench.tsx` is now a ~480-line composition root that owns all state/effects (fetch-once + polling loops, boot sequence, ⌘K keybind, `runCf`/`decideHeal` — kept here because the command palette calls `runCf` too) and wires up the extracted pieces. Shell chrome moved to `frontend/src/workbench/{WorkbenchBoot,WorkbenchTopbar,WorkbenchNav,CommandPalette,Toast}.tsx` plus a shared `navConfig.ts` (nav groups/icons, used by both the rail and the palette's "Go to X" commands) and `bootStages.ts`. Each cockpit board card became its own file under `frontend/src/workbench/cockpit/`: `CockpitStats`, `LiveRadarPanel`, `AskAuraChat` (owns its own message/thinking state and the `ask()`/`describeChatError` logic), `ForensicAuditPanel` (+ `cfStages.ts`; owns the `audience` toggle, takes `cf`/`runCf` as props), `HealingQueueApprovals`, `PipelinesStreamingPanel` (derives its run-rows locally from a `pipelines` prop), `LineageSummaryCard`, `QueryHistoryTable`, and `SessionEventsFeed`, with shared row/state shapes in `cockpit/types.ts`. No visual or behavioral change — all extraction, no new props beyond what wiring the pieces back together required. Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src --max-warnings 0`, `npx vitest run` (67 files / 311 tests, including all 11 in `Workbench.test.tsx`) clean from `frontend/`.

## BUG-030: file upload never wrote a dataset profile, so GET /files/{id}/profile always 404'd
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** live re-run of the expanded `scripts/verify_live_deployment.py` against `https://dataaura.duckdns.org` after landing the codebase-quality-audit fixes (BUG-016..029), 2026-09-02 — the new `file_upload_profile` check (added alongside BUG-016/017) failed with a live 404, exactly the "promised but not wired" gap that check's own author flagged as likely when writing it.
- **Severity:** production-gap — `deploy/aws-free-tier/README.md` promises "File upload → profile → query" as working on this deployment; `GET /files/{file_id}/profile` (`aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/files.py`) is fully implemented on the read side, but nothing on the write side ever called it for a plain upload.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/metadata_store/repository.py::upsert_dataset_profile` is only ever called from `api_gateway/routers/connections.py` (the external-DB connector sync) and the pipeline's semantic-model route — `upload_universal` (`files.py`'s `/upload` handler) never called it, so a profile row for a plain-uploaded file never existed and the read route's `repo.get_dataset_profile(file_id)` always returned `None` → 404.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing since the plain-upload endpoint and the profile-read endpoint were added independently, never wired together.
- **Fix:** added a backgrounded, best-effort hook to `upload_universal` (`aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/files.py`), matching the existing `schema_indexer`/`file_metadata` hooks already in the same function: computes the file's schema via `pipeline.generator.PipelineGenerator.get_file_schema` (offloaded via `asyncio.to_thread` — it does blocking DuckDB I/O) and writes it through `repo.upsert_dataset_profile`, dispatched via `shared.tasks.fire_and_forget`.
  Test: `aurabackend/tests/test_files_upload_dataset_profile_bug030.py` — monkeypatches `metadata_store.repository.get_repository` and `shared.tasks.fire_and_forget` directly (a recording fake, no real DB/engine/lifespan/background-task-registry involved) rather than exercising the real stack end-to-end. That path was tried first and hit three real, pre-existing test-infrastructure issues, none of them this fix's concern (each already independently covered: `get_file_schema` by BUG-020's own tests, `upsert_dataset_profile` by `test_metadata_store.py`) —
  1. A prior sync `TestClient` test in the same pytest process leaves `metadata_store`'s module-level async engine's aiosqlite connections bound to a now-closed event loop, raising "Event loop is closed" from a background thread on reuse — the same aiosqlite/event-loop class BUG-008 already fixed elsewhere.
  2. `httpx.ASGITransport` does not drive FastAPI's lifespan, so `api_gateway/main.py`'s startup call to `metadata_store.db.init_db()` never runs — a freshly-reset engine has no tables until a test calls `init_db()` itself.
  3. `api_gateway/routers/workspaces.py::_UPLOADS_ROOT` is read from `AURA_UPLOADS_ROOT` once at module import time, not per-request — fine in production (the env var never changes after process start) but means `monkeypatch.setenv("AURA_UPLOADS_ROOT", ...)` is a no-op for `tenant_upload_dir()` once that module has already been imported by an earlier test in the same process.
  Worth knowing for any future async test that touches `metadata_store`'s real engine or `AURA_UPLOADS_ROOT`-derived paths. 8/8 tests pass in `test_files_storage.py` + this file together, 60/60 across the broader related suite (`test_metadata_store.py`, `test_pipeline_execution.py` included).
  Live re-verification post-merge initially still showed `file_upload_profile` failing — traced to `scripts/verify_live_deployment.py`'s own check asserting on the profile immediately after upload with zero retry, racing the intentionally-backgrounded write; a direct manual `curl` upload+profile round-trip (with a real delay) confirmed the production fix itself works correctly. Fixed the check to poll (10× 0.5s) instead of asserting on the first response. 14/14 checks now pass live against `https://dataaura.duckdns.org`.

## BUG-031: the live box's automated redeploy has been silently failing every cycle since BUG-015's cron was set up
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** live re-verification after merging BUG-030 — the box was still serving a build from before PR #284 (the redeploy-automation PR itself), despite ~2 hours and 8+ cron cycles having passed since.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (deploy pipeline) — this defeats the entire point of BUG-015's fix: the box silently stopped auto-updating again, the same failure class BUG-015 was meant to close, just with a different specific cause.
- **Root cause:** `deploy/aws-free-tier/redeploy.sh` was `scp`'d directly onto the box as an *untracked* file while building BUG-015's fix, before that file existed in the repo. Once PR #284 merged the same path into git, every subsequent `git checkout origin/main` inside the cron-scheduled `redeploy.sh` itself failed with `error: The following untracked working tree files would be overwritten by checkout` — the untracked copy blocked git from ever checking out the very commit that would have replaced it with a tracked one. `redeploy.log` showed this exact error on every single 15-minute run since the file was first scp'd, with no operator ever seeing it (a cron job's stdout isn't watched).
- **Caused by:** none — a self-inflicted gap in how BUG-015's fix was rolled out mid-session (scp-before-merge), not a defect in the redeploy design itself.
- **Fix:** removed the untracked file on the box (confirmed byte-identical to the tracked version first via `diff`, so nothing was lost), ran `git checkout origin/main` manually to catch the box up to `f3e7e54` (12 commits behind), `docker compose pull && up -d` to bring images current, and `chmod +x` (git's checkout hadn't yet re-applied the tracked `100755` mode bit before the manual restore). Re-ran `redeploy.sh` once more afterward and confirmed it now completes cleanly and idempotently (`Running`, not `Recreate`, on containers with nothing to change). No code change was needed — the fix was operational (remove the stray file); the script itself was always correct once nothing blocked its `git checkout`.
- **Note:** the same "recover the box, then verify the fix by re-running the script itself" discipline BUG-015 established was followed here too — this closes with the redeploy mechanism actually proven working, not just assumed working from the earlier session's test.

## BUG-032: GET /uasr/deployment never reported whether UASR_SCHEMA_INTENT_ENABLED is armed — the exact BUG-013 pattern recurring for the newest opt-in
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** uasr gap-analysis re-run, sprint-backlog-and-uasr-gaps workflow, 2026-09-03.
- **Severity:** degrades-accuracy — an operator has no way to confirm, via the one endpoint built for exactly this purpose, whether an operator/CI migration step's `POST /uasr/schema-intent` calls will actually take effect (as opposed to 400ing) without either reading the env directly on the box or triggering a live `POST /uasr/schema-intent` and checking for the 400.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/uasr/service.py:90-99` resolves `_SCHEMA_INTENT_ENABLED` from `UASR_SCHEMA_INTENT_ENABLED` and passes it into the `DriftDetector`, but neither `deployment_summary()` (`aurabackend/uasr/runtime_config.py:236-273` — the single function every other opt-in flag is threaded through: `numeric_semantics`, `numeric_auto_heal`, `martingale_detector`, `shim_router`, `causal_rl_evaluator`, `post_heal_validation_batches`, `approval_timeout_seconds`, `correlation_window_seconds`/`min_sources`/`auto_heal`) nor `uasr_deployment()` (`service.py:1155-1168`, which augments that summary with `repair_backend_class`/`state_store_class`) ever surfaced `_SCHEMA_INTENT_ENABLED` or `_SCHEMA_INTENT_DEFAULT_TTL_SECONDS`.
- **Caused by:** none — schema-evolution-intent shipped in S51 (PR #276) after BUG-013 had already documented this exact "implemented but never armed, no signal via `GET /uasr/deployment`" failure mode for approval-timeout/per-tenant-fairness in the same sprint; the observability gap was simply never closed for the flag that landed after the lesson was learned.
- **Fix:** `uasr_deployment()` in `aurabackend/uasr/service.py` now sets `summary["schema_intent_enabled"] = _SCHEMA_INTENT_ENABLED` and `summary["schema_intent_default_ttl_seconds"] = _SCHEMA_INTENT_DEFAULT_TTL_SECONDS` before returning.
  Test: `aurabackend/tests/test_uasr_schema_intent.py::TestDeploymentSummaryReportsSchemaIntent` — reuses this file's existing `importlib.reload(uasr.service)` pattern (the flag is resolved once at module-import time) to set `UASR_SCHEMA_INTENT_ENABLED` true/false via `monkeypatch`, calls `uasr_deployment()` directly (no ASGI lifespan, per BUG-008), and asserts `schema_intent_enabled`/`schema_intent_default_ttl_seconds` reflect it. 22/22 tests pass in `test_uasr_schema_intent.py`.

## BUG-034: Hᵤ trend/decline alerts are driven by how often /uasr/metrics or /uasr/metrics/alerts happens to be polled, not by real elapsed time
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** uasr gap-analysis re-run, sprint-backlog-and-uasr-gaps workflow, 2026-09-03
- **Severity:** cosmetic
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/uasr/metrics.py::HealingMetricTracker.compute()` (lines 305-431, pre-fix) unconditionally appended a new sample to `self._hu_history`/`self._composite_history` (lines 409-411, pre-fix) on every call, with no time-based gating. Both `GET /uasr/metrics` (`aurabackend/uasr/service.py:958-984`, calls `_tracker.compute()`) and `GET /uasr/metrics/alerts` (`service.py:1013-1017`, calls `_tracker.check_alerts()`, which itself calls `self.compute()` at `metrics.py:446`) share this one method, so every poll from every consumer — a dashboard refreshing every few seconds, a separate alerting bot polling on its own cadence, a human curling the endpoint — added a trend sample. The "Hᵤ has been declining for the last 5 computations" alert (`metrics.py:511-520`) reads `self._hu_history[-5:]` and fires on strict monotonic decline across those 5 *calls*, not 5 real time periods, so its true detection window shrank or grew arbitrarily with whatever mix of clients happened to be polling — a dashboard polling once a minute alongside an alerting script polling every 10 seconds meant the "5-reading" trend check was actually looking at roughly the last 50 seconds from whichever source polled last, and adding or removing a poller silently changed what the alert meant without any config change.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing since the trend/alert mechanism was first added.
- **Fix:** decoupled trend-history recording from ad-hoc `compute()` calls. Added a `trend_min_interval_seconds` constructor param (default `30.0`) and a `self._last_trend_recorded_at` wall-clock timestamp; `compute()` now only appends to `_hu_history`/`_composite_history` when at least that interval has elapsed since the last recorded sample, while still returning a freshly computed report on every call regardless (`aurabackend/uasr/metrics.py`, `__init__`/`compute()`/`reset()`). The "5 computations" trend/alert window is now genuinely ~5 × `trend_min_interval_seconds` of real time, independent of poll frequency or poller count.
  Test: `aurabackend/tests/test_uasr.py` — `test_trend_not_recorded_within_min_interval` (two `compute()` calls 5s apart, under the 30s default, extend `_hu_history` only once), `test_trend_recorded_after_min_interval_elapses` (a call 31s later does extend it), and `test_hu_trend_alert_uses_wall_clock_samples_not_poll_count` (10 rapid `check_alerts()` polls 1s apart record at most one trend sample, not ten) — all via `monkeypatch.setattr(time, "time", ...)` for determinism. `../.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest tests/test_uasr.py --tb=short`: 29/29 pass. Related suites (`test_uasr_metrics_drift_type_attribution.py`, `test_uasr_hu_decoupling.py`, `test_uasr_cross_source_correlation.py`) unaffected: 30/30 pass.

## BUG-033: Martingale-detected drift always synthesizes a no-op pass-through shim that vacuously passes post-heal validation and auto-deploys as "fixed"
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** uasr gap-analysis re-run, sprint-backlog-and-uasr-gaps workflow, 2026-09-03
- **Severity:** blocks-feature
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/uasr/mapek_worker.py:575-637` (`_analyze_martingale`) built `drift_vector={"detector": "wasserstein_martingale", "alpha": ..., "alarms": ..., "max_wasserstein_1": ...}` — it never populated `affected_columns`, `col_stats`, `max_kl`, or `threshold_zeta`. When this drift routed to the actuator, `_statistical_shim` (`aurabackend/uasr/actuator_agent.py:245-355`) read `affected = drift_vector.get("affected_columns", [])` (empty, since martingale used the differently-named `alarms` key — the classical detector at `drift_detector.py:274-379,583` always sets `affected_columns` inside its own `drift_vector`, so this was the format every actuator code path assumed) and `col_stats = drift_vector.get("col_stats", {})` (always empty), so the deterministic rescale-detection loop (lines 260-277) never fired; `max_kl = drift_vector.get("max_kl", 0)` (always 0) meant `if max_kl > zeta * 5` (line 300) was always False. Execution fell into the "Mild - just log and pass through" branch (lines 338-355), returning a shim whose `transform()` returns `rows` unchanged. Because `_template_shim` returned a non-None shim, `SynthesisActuatorAgent._run` (`actuator_agent.py:86-89`) never fell through to LLM generation — the no-op was committed as `generation_method="template"`. `RecoveryLoop._validate_shim` (`recovery_loop.py:366-461`) then re-ran the classical detector (never the martingale detector that actually fired) against the byte-identical rows, frequently reporting "Drift fully resolved" for a shim that changed zero rows. Under `UASR_RISK_TIERED=true` + AUTO mode this auto-deployed (MEDIUM severity + `generation_method=template` is exactly the tier `_should_deploy`, `recovery_loop.py:522-544`, auto-approves) while the martingale-flagged drift went untouched.
- **Caused by:** none — a gap in S18.1's original martingale integration (the martingale path was wired to produce the same `DriftDetectionResult` *shape* as the classical detector at the top level, but its nested `drift_vector` payload was never brought up to the contract the actuator's statistical-shim templates actually depend on).
- **Fix:** `aurabackend/uasr/martingale.py` — added `WassersteinMartingaleDetector.baseline_stats(source_id, column) -> (mean, std)`, a public accessor over the previously-private `self._baselines[source_id][column]` sample list. `aurabackend/uasr/mapek_worker.py::_analyze_martingale` now builds, for every alarmed column, `col_stats[col] = {"baseline_mean": ..., "batch_mean": ..., "baseline_std": ...}` from that accessor and the per-column `samples` list already collected in the detection loop, and also sets `drift_vector["affected_columns"] = alarms` (mirroring the classical detector's shape so `_statistical_shim`'s existing `affected_columns`-driven logic — both the rescale heal and the fallback branch below — actually runs instead of short-circuiting on an always-empty list). With `col_stats` populated, the existing deterministic rescale-detection in `_statistical_shim` (`actuator_agent.py:260-277`) now fires correctly for genuine unit-bug drift caught by martingale. `aurabackend/uasr/actuator_agent.py::_statistical_shim` — the "Mild - just log and pass through" fallback now returns `None` instead of a no-op shim whenever `drift_vector.get("detector") == "wasserstein_martingale"` (i.e. a real martingale-only shift with no clean rescale factor), so `_template_shim` returns `None` and `SynthesisActuatorAgent._run` correctly falls through to LLM/fallback generation instead of committing a vacuous template.
  Tests: `aurabackend/tests/test_mapek_martingale_integration.py` — `test_martingale_rescale_drift_produces_col_stats_and_real_shim` drives a synthetic x100 unit-scale-bug batch through the live martingale detector end-to-end, asserts `col_stats`/`affected_columns` land in the resulting `drift_vector`, and asserts the actuator's `_statistical_shim` on that vector produces a "Unit Rescale" shim whose compiled `transform()` actually divides the affected column back to baseline units (not a pass-through). `test_martingale_non_rescalable_drift_falls_through_to_llm_not_noop` asserts a martingale drift_vector with `col_stats` present but no clean rescale factor makes `_template_shim` return `None` (falls through to LLM/fallback generation instead of auto-deploying a no-op). 11/11 pass in that file; 66/66 pass across `test_uasr_actuator_shims.py` + `test_uasr_shim_healing.py` + `test_uasr.py` + `test_uasr_causal_rl.py` (no regression in the classical-detector or existing template-shim paths).

## BUG-035: etl.py's and pipelines.py's source-file reads bypass the S45 StorageBackend abstraction — 404 under AURA_STORAGE_BACKEND=s3
- **Status:** fixed — `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/etl.py` (previous pass) and `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/pipelines.py` + `aurabackend/pipeline/engine.py` + `aurabackend/pipeline/generator.py` + the `files.py` upload dataset-profile hook (this pass) all now route through the S45 StorageBackend abstraction.
- **Found by:** S45's own PR description (#117) flagged this as a known follow-up ("etl.py/pipelines.py still read the tenant dir directly, local-only under S3"); confirmed still true and picked up 2026-09-03.
- **Severity:** production-gap — `POST /etl/preview-source`, `POST /etl/execute`, `POST /etl/natural-language`, `POST /pipeline/generate`, `POST /pipeline/execute`, `POST /pipeline/execute/async`, and `GET /pipeline/schema/{file_name}` all built a local filesystem path via `tenant_upload_dir(request)` + a raw/sandboxed join and read it directly, instead of going through `shared/storage`'s `StorageBackend` abstraction (S45, PR #117) the way `files.py`/`data_utils.py` already do. Under `AURA_STORAGE_BACKEND=local` (the default) this was invisible — the resolved path happened to match where `LocalBackend` also looks. Under `AURA_STORAGE_BACKEND=s3`, `POST /upload` writes the file to S3 only (`files.py:160`), so these endpoints found nothing on local disk and either 404'd or (for the pipeline routes, whose engine swallows source-load errors onto the run object) silently reported a failed run for a file a caller had legitimately just uploaded. Separately, `files.py`'s `POST /upload` dataset-profile background hook called `PipelineGenerator.get_file_schema(safe_name, tenant_upload_dir(request))` — once `get_file_schema` was moved onto the tenant-keyed StorageBackend contract, passing it a local directory path instead of a bare tenant made every such lookup miss, so no upload ever recorded a profile.
- **Caused by:** none — a known S45 follow-up (its own PR description named exactly this gap), not a regression from any later change.
- **Fix (etl.py, previous pass):** `etl_preview_source`, `etl_execute`, and `etl_from_natural_language` now resolve the source file via `get_storage_backend().exists(tenant, name)` / `.duckdb_uri(tenant, name)` instead of `safe_join(upload_dir, name)` + `Path.exists()` — the same read pattern `data_utils.py::build_schema_context` already uses. Path-traversal protection moved from `safe_join`/`PathTraversalError` to the backend's own `safe_object_name()` (rejects absolute paths, `..`, separators, NUL) — same guarantee, enforced at the layer the backend itself owns.
- **Fix (pipelines.py + pipeline/engine.py + pipeline/generator.py, this pass):** Mirrors the etl.py pattern, threaded through the engine/generator's own public signatures (not just their router callers), replacing every `upload_dir: str` parameter with `tenant: Optional[str]`:
  - `pipeline/engine.py::PipelineEngine.execute()` now builds its DuckDB connection via `shared.duckdb_factory.new_connection()` (not a bare `duckdb.connect`) so the active backend's `configure_duckdb()` (httpfs + S3 secret) runs before any source read. `_load_source()`/`_load_file_source()` take `tenant` and resolve through `get_storage_backend().exists()/.duckdb_uri()`, `safe_object_name()` replacing the removed `_resolve_in_dir()` traversal guard.
  - `_load_duckdb_source()`'s external-database-file branch (`source.connection["database"]`) computes `backend.duckdb_uri(tenant, safe_name)` and checks it for `"://"` **before** calling `backend.exists()` — a pure string-format check with zero I/O for any backend — so under `AURA_STORAGE_BACKEND=s3` it raises a clear "not supported (deferred)" `ValueError` without ever making a network call, instead of resolving through `exists()`/ATTACH first and hitting a "file not found" or (with real credentials) a partial-download of a file DuckDB's ATTACH can't stream anyway. DuckDB's ATTACH has no S3-range-read equivalent of `read_csv_auto()` for a whole external `.duckdb` file, so this stays a documented deferral, not a bug — use a FILE source under S3 mode instead.
  - `pipeline/generator.py::PipelineGenerator.generate()`/`._discover_files()`/`.get_file_schema()` take `tenant` the same way; `_discover_files()` now lists via `get_storage_backend().list(tenant)` instead of scanning a local directory. Tradeoff (accepted, not a defect): `StorageBackend.list()` only returns the extensions each backend indexes for reading (`.csv`/`.parquet`/`.json`) — `.xlsx`/`.tsv`, which the old local scan also surfaced, are no longer auto-discovered; widening that is a `shared/storage` change out of scope here.
  - `api_gateway/routers/pipelines.py`'s four affected endpoints now resolve `tenant = _request_tenant(request)` (not `tenant_upload_dir(request)`) and pass it straight through.
  - `api_gateway/routers/inbound_hooks.py::_fire_pipeline` (the webhook-triggered execution path) has no `Request` to read a tenant from. New additive helpers close that: `api_gateway/persistence.py::get_pipeline_workspace_id(pipeline_id)` looks up just the owning `workspace_id` column (without loading the full definition JSON, which carries no such field), and `api_gateway/routers/workspaces.py::tenant_from_workspace_id(workspace_id)` recovers the tenant by splitting on `current_workspace_id()`'s `"<tenant>::<folder>"` format. **Documented residual gap:** an unauthenticated caller's workspace id is the raw client-supplied `X-Workspace-Id` header (or `"default"`), which does not identify the storage tenant a file was uploaded under (an unauthenticated upload always lands in the shared `"default"` bucket regardless of that header) — a pipeline saved under a custom unauthenticated workspace folder and triggered via webhook recovers the wrong tenant here. Narrow enough (fully-open deployment + non-default workspace folder + webhook trigger) that it needs its own design pass on `workspace_id` itself rather than a fix folded into this one.
  - `api_gateway/routers/files.py`'s `POST /upload` dataset-profile hook (`_record_upload_profile`) now takes `tenant: Optional[str]` and is dispatched with `_request_tenant(request)` (already imported) instead of `tenant_upload_dir(request)`, matching `get_file_schema`'s new tenant-keyed contract. The separate `refresh_schema_context([upload_dir])` call a few lines above is untouched — it is not a `PipelineGenerator`/`PipelineEngine` call site.
  - `api_gateway/routers/chat.py`'s Commander-mode pipeline-intent block (`POST /chat`, `intent == "pipeline"`, ~line 407-425) had the identical `os.listdir(tenant_upload_dir(...))` pattern and was named in-scope by the original design but dropped by the implementer (a bug in this fix's own orchestration: when the design-review verdict was "changes-needed", the implementer received only the review's corrections text, not the full original design, and had no other record of chat.py being in scope). Caught in post-implementation review and fixed directly: `available_files` now comes from `get_storage_backend().list(tenant)`, and `tenant` is passed through to `gen.generate()` (previously omitted entirely).
- **Tests:** `aurabackend/tests/test_etl_preview_source_traversal.py` (existing traversal regression, isolates the storage backend via `AURA_UPLOADS_ROOT` + `reset_storage_backend()`) — 1/1 pass. `aurabackend/tests/test_etl_storage_backend.py` (Tier A, local mode) — 3/3 pass. `aurabackend/tests/test_pipeline_execution.py` (rewritten onto `tenant=`/StorageBackend fixtures; adds `test_duckdb_source_missing_file_reports_failed_run` and `test_duckdb_source_s3_deferral_raises_before_any_network_call` — the latter points the S3 endpoint at a closed local port so a regressed check-order would fail on a connection error instead of the intended `ValueError`, proving the deferral path makes zero network calls) — 8/8 pass. New `aurabackend/tests/test_pipeline_storage_backend.py` (Tier A, local mode): `/pipeline/execute` reads an uploaded file via the backend and produces correct aggregated results; an unknown file reports a failed run (not a 500); `/pipeline/schema/{file}` and `/pipeline/generate`'s explicit-`source_file` schema lookup both read via the backend; `test_upload_dataset_profile_reflects_real_uploaded_columns` drives the real `/upload` route end-to-end (isolated in-memory metadata store) and asserts the recorded dataset profile has the uploaded file's real columns/row count — the direct regression proof for the `files.py` fix — 5/5 pass. New Tier-B `test_pipeline_execute_reads_uploaded_file_from_s3` in `aurabackend/tests/test_storage_s3_duckdb.py` (gated on `AURA_S3_TEST_ENDPOINT`, runs in the existing `storage-s3` CI lane against real MinIO) drives `/pipeline/schema/{file}` and `/pipeline/execute` end-to-end against an S3-backed upload — this is the actual regression proof for the pipelines.py half of the bug; could not be run locally (no MinIO daemon available in this environment) but will execute in CI, alongside the pre-existing `test_etl_preview_and_execute_read_uploaded_file_from_s3`. `aurabackend/tests/test_inbound_hooks.py` (27/27) and `aurabackend/tests/test_tenant_isolation.py` + `test_api_gateway_persistence.py` (53/53) re-run clean, confirming no regression in the webhook-trigger or persistence paths. New `aurabackend/tests/test_chat_commander_pipeline_storage_backend.py` (Tier A, local mode) drives `POST /chat` end-to-end with `IntentAgent.execute` monkeypatched to force `intent="pipeline"` (deterministic, no dependency on real LLM classification) and asserts `PipelineGenerator.generate()` receives `available_files` from the backend and a real `tenant` — 1/1 pass.
## BUG-036: GET /synthetic/jobs and GET /synthetic/jobs/{job_id} have zero tenant scoping — any caller can list/read another tenant's synthetic-data job
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** `tenant-isolation-audit` workflow, 2026-09-03 (audit phase completed cleanly; verify/implement phases were heavily disrupted by a session usage-limit wall mid-run, so this finding was personally re-verified against the actual code and fixed directly rather than trusted from the workflow's own partial pipeline).
- **Severity:** blocks-feature — `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/synthetic.py`'s `_jobs` is a bare module-level `Dict[str, Dict]` (line 41) with no tenant field of any kind. `list_synthetic_jobs` (`GET /synthetic/jobs`) returned every tenant's jobs (output URIs, schema definitions, target sizes) unfiltered; `get_synthetic_job` (`GET /synthetic/jobs/{job_id}`) had no ownership check at all, so any authenticated caller could poll another tenant's job by guessing/enumerating its 12-hex-char id.
- **Root cause:** `synthetic_generate` (`POST /synthetic/generate`) never stamped the job record with any caller identity when it wrote `_jobs[job_id] = {...}`; the two read routes correspondingly had no key to filter or check against. Same bare-in-memory-store, no-tenant-column pattern as BUG-016 (webhooks/inbound_hooks) and the `synthetic.py` findings mirror it exactly.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing since the router was added, not a regression from any recent change.
- **Fix:** `synthetic_generate`/`list_synthetic_jobs`/`get_synthetic_job` now take `Request` and use `current_workspace_id(request)` (the same chokepoint `pipelines.py`/`dashboards.py`/`connections.py` already use). The job record gains a `workspace_id` field stamped at creation; `list_synthetic_jobs` filters to it; `get_synthetic_job` reports the same 404 for a cross-tenant id as a genuinely unknown one, never confirming the job exists under a different tenant.
  Test: new `aurabackend/tests/test_synthetic_tenant_isolation.py` — drives the route functions directly against a fake `Request` carrying a verified `org_id` (mirrors `tests/test_tenant_isolation.py`'s `_req` pattern): list is scoped per workspace, cross-tenant get 404s, and `generate` stamps the caller's workspace on the new job. 3/3 pass. Existing `tests/test_synthetic_api.py` (7/7) re-run clean, no regression.

## BUG-037: OIDC SSO login-flow routes missing from JWTAuthMiddleware's public-path allowlist — SSO unreachable on any deployment with JWT armed
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** `tenant-isolation-audit` workflow, 2026-09-03 (see BUG-036's note on the workflow's partial disruption; independently re-verified before fixing).
- **Severity:** blocks-feature — `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/auth.py`'s four OIDC routes (`GET /auth/oidc/status`, `GET /auth/oidc/login`, `GET /auth/oidc/callback`, `POST /auth/oidc/exchange`) are each coded to be reached by a browser that has no AURA JWT yet: `oidc_login` redirects to the IdP before any AURA login exists, `oidc_callback` is hit by the IdP's redirect carrying only a fresh SSO state cookie, `oidc_exchange` is the very call that mints the caller's first AURA token, and `oidc_status` is polled by the login page to decide whether to show an SSO button. None of the four appeared in `_PUBLIC_PATHS`/`_PUBLIC_PATH_PREFIXES` (`shared/middleware.py`). `jwt_enabled` is validator-enforced mandatory in production (`shared/config.py`'s `_require_jwt_for_tenant_isolation_in_production`), so `JWTAuthMiddleware` 401'd every one of these routes before the handler ever ran on every production deployment — the exact same class of gap as BUG-005 (`/counterfactual/jwks`/`audit/sth`) and BUG-017 (`/hooks/fire/{slug}`), both times a correctly-coded intentionally-unauthenticated handler shipped with the allowlist never catching up.
- **Caused by:** none — a known-class gap (third recurrence of the BUG-005/017 pattern), not a regression from any recent change.
- **Fix:** added the four `/api/v1/auth/oidc/*` paths to `_PUBLIC_PATHS` in `shared/middleware.py`. Also added `oidc_callback`/`oidc_exchange` (but not `oidc_status`/`oidc_login`, which mint no credential) to `_AUTH_PATHS`, so they're excluded from the rate-limit exemption `_PUBLIC_PATHS` otherwise grants — both mint a real AURA credential from caller-supplied input, the same class of endpoint `/auth/token` already is, and should be throttled the same way rather than treated as free infrastructure traffic.
  Test: `aurabackend/tests/test_middleware.py` — four new OIDC stub routes added to the shared `_make_app()` test fixture (mirroring the existing BUG-005/BUG-017 stub pattern) plus four new `TestJWTMiddleware` cases asserting each is reachable without a token with `JWTAuthMiddleware` genuinely installed (not the "auth defaults off" gap that let BUG-005 ship undetected). 37/37 pass across `test_middleware.py` (26) + `test_collab.py` (11, unrelated but re-run together).

## BUG-038: collab.py's WebSocket relay has zero authentication and zero tenant scoping; GET /collab/rooms leaks every tenant's room list
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** `tenant-isolation-audit` workflow, 2026-09-03 (see BUG-036's note on the workflow's partial disruption; independently re-verified before fixing).
- **Severity:** blocks-feature — `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/collab.py`'s `WS /ws/collab/{room_id}` (the real-time Yjs CRDT relay backing collaborative editing) had no auth check of any kind, and the module's own docstring documented this as a deliberate v1 punt: "WS auth (token in query string) is a v2 follow-up because the existing JWT middleware short-circuits on the `/ws/` prefix today." `JWTAuthMiddleware` is a `BaseHTTPMiddleware` subclass, which only intercepts `scope["type"] == "http"` and structurally never runs for WebSocket connections — so this was true regardless of any HTTP-level allowlist entry. Any caller who knew or guessed a `room_id` could join it and both read and inject arbitrary edits into another tenant's live document. Separately, `GET /collab/rooms` (`list_rooms`) returned every tenant's active room ids and occupancy counts unfiltered — a real HTTP route, correctly gated by `JWTAuthMiddleware` when armed, but with no `current_workspace_id(request)` filter on the underlying `_rooms` dict, unlike every other list endpoint in this codebase.
- **Root cause:** `_rooms: Dict[str, _Room]` is keyed purely by the caller-supplied `room_id` string with no tenant component, and `collab_socket`'s only gate was `websocket.accept()` — no dependency, no token check, no read of any verified identity before joining the room and relaying.
- **Caused by:** none — a documented, deliberate v1 scope decision (not a silent oversight) that had not yet had its own v2 follow-up done; this closes it.
- **Fix:** implements the v2 plan the v1 docstring already specified. The auth token now travels as a `?token=` query-string param (a WebSocket handshake can't carry a bearer `Authorization` header) and is decoded with the same `shared.auth.decode_access_token` the HTTP routes use, checked **before** `websocket.accept()` so an invalid/missing token rejects the handshake outright rather than accepting then immediately closing. Gated on `settings.jwt_enabled` — open/dev mode keeps v1's original ungated behavior (every caller shares a `"default"` tenant namespace, matching the rest of the app's unauthenticated fallback). Rooms are now stored internally as `"<tenant>:<room_id>"` so two tenants can never collide on the same room_id string; `GET /collab/rooms` filters `_rooms` to the caller's own tenant prefix (via the existing `_request_tenant(request)` helper) and strips the prefix back off in the response.
  Tests: `aurabackend/tests/test_collab.py` — all 7 existing tests re-run unchanged (open/dev mode's `"default"` namespace round-trips transparently through the prefix-strip). 4 new tests with a dedicated `jwt_client` fixture (the base `client` fixture deliberately mounts no middleware, so a separate fixture installs `JWTAuthMiddleware` for real): connection rejected with no token, rejected with an invalid token, accepted with a valid token, and `GET /collab/rooms` proven to isolate two tenants using the **identical** room_id string (each sees one room with 1 occupant, not one shared room with 2 — the direct proof rooms don't collide across tenants). 11/11 pass.
## BUG-040: cross-tenant HITL exception decision — auditor role check has no tenant/ownership scoping
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** tenant-isolation-audit-followup workflow, 2026-09-03.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (security) — any authenticated caller with role `auditor` or `admin`, regardless of tenant/org, could record a binding, signed WORM `audit_human_override` decision against ANY other tenant's financial-audit finding, given only that tenant's `record_hash` (a 64-char hex string one can obtain from e.g. a shared link or log line). A malicious or compromised auditor account in one org could approve or override another org's PCAOB AS 1215 exception findings.
- **Root cause:** `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/counterfactual.py:239-244`'s `financial_audit_decide` route is gated only by `Depends(_require_auditor)` (`aurabackend/counterfactual_service/main.py:814-821`) — a role check with no tenant/ownership comparison — and forwards to the service layer with no tenant argument. The service-layer `financial_audit_decide` (`main.py:835-849`) called `exception_queue.record_decision(record_hash, finding_id, user["sub"], req.rationale, req.approved)` without passing tenant, even though the file already has the established helper `_ledger_tenant(user)` and already uses it two call sites away (`_append_review_to_ledger(tenant_id=_ledger_tenant(user), ...)` at line 863, and again in the sibling `financial_audit` function at line 694). `exception_queue.record_decision` (lines 84-116) loaded the report via `_load_report(report_hash)` → `persistence.read_artifact(report_hash)` but never read/compared `report.get("tenant_id")`, even though `build_completion_document` (`financial_report.py:70`) stamps `"tenant_id": tenant_id` onto every report dict at creation time — the field was there and simply never checked on the write path.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing since the S34b HITL exception queue was added; this is a distinct trust boundary from the deliberately tenant-unscoped read/verify surface (`/audit/financial/verify/{record_hash}`, `pending_exceptions`, and the bulk-replay endpoint), which is documented, content-addressed-hash-as-read-capability behavior and stays unscoped by design.
- **Fix:** `exception_queue.record_decision` takes a new optional `tenant_id: str | None = None` keyword. When set, it compares `report.get("tenant_id")` against it immediately after loading the report and raises the same `LookupError("no completion document for {report_hash}")` a genuinely unknown report/finding_id already raises — an ownership mismatch reports identically to "not found," never confirming a cross-tenant `record_hash` exists (same pattern as BUG-016's read-path precedent). `pending_exceptions()` in the same file is deliberately left unchanged — that GET path's trust boundary is possession of the hash, not tenant identity. `main.py::financial_audit_decide` now passes `tenant_id=_ledger_tenant(user)` into `record_decision`; the existing `except (LookupError, ValueError) as exc: raise HTTPException(404, str(exc))` handles the new cross-tenant case with no further change. Fixed the pre-existing `test_exception_endpoints_e2e` fixture (`auditor = {"sub": "auditor-7", "role": "auditor"}`, no `org_id`) to `org_id: "t1"` — same-tenant as the report it decides — since it only passed before because nothing checked tenant at all; once the fix lands, `_ledger_tenant` on an `org_id`-less dict falls back to `sub` ("auditor-7"), which no longer matches the report's "t1" and would 404 a legitimate same-org decision.
- **Tests:** `aurabackend/tests/test_exception_queue.py` — new `test_record_decision_rejects_cross_tenant_auditor` (service-layer: cross-tenant `record_decision` call raises `LookupError`; a same-tenant call on the identical finding still succeeds) and `test_exception_decide_rejects_cross_tenant_auditor_e2e` (drives the actual `financial_audit_decide` FastAPI route function with an `org_id="t2"` auditor against a `tenant_id="t1"` report, asserts `HTTPException` with `status_code == 404`). 12/12 pass in `test_exception_queue.py`; 36/36 pass across `test_exception_queue.py` + `test_counterfactual_sprint9.py` + `test_counterfactual_sprint13.py` (no regression in the unscoped-by-design read/verify/bulk-replay paths).
## BUG-039: gateway_query_history has no workspace_id column — every tenant's query history is readable and writable by any authenticated caller
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** tenant-isolation-audit-followup workflow, 2026-09-03
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (a cross-tenant confidentiality leak, same class as BUG-016/018/019 — the entire point of the workspace boundary is defeated for this table)
- **Root cause:** `QueryHistoryRow` (`aurabackend/api_gateway/persistence.py:86-108`, pre-fix) was the one table in this module with no `workspace_id` column at all — unlike its sibling `SavedQueryRow`, which has carried one since Sprint P-1. `list_query_history` (`persistence.py:600-615`) ran a bare `select(QueryHistoryRow)` with no tenant filter, and `GET /query-history` (`api_gateway/routers/queries.py:470-478`, pre-fix) didn't even accept a `Request` to scope from — any authenticated caller could read up to 200 other tenants' executed SQL/prompts via `persistence.list_query_history`'s 200-row global cap. `POST /query-history` (`save_query_history`, `queries.py:897-911`, pre-fix) and the shared `track_query` helper (`queries.py:155-183`, called by `chat.py`'s `_track_query` on every executed chat query) wrote rows with no owning tenant at all, so `insert_query_history` (`persistence.py:565-597`) never had a tenant to stamp even in principle. Same shape as BUG-019 (evolution subsystem): a table that never got tenanted, and route handlers that couldn't scope even if they wanted to because they took no `Request`.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing since Sprint P-1 first introduced `gateway_query_history`; `SavedQueryRow` got `workspace_id` in that same sprint but `QueryHistoryRow` was missed.
- **Fix:** new migration `aurabackend/alembic/versions/20260903_e5f6a7b8c9d0_query_history_workspace_id.py` (chains off `d4e5f6a7b8c9`, the confirmed alembic head at fix time) adds a nullable, indexed `workspace_id` column to `gateway_query_history` — nullable per the same "pre-tenanting rows become invisible, not universally visible" fail-closed direction as BUG-019's migration, not backfilled. `persistence.py`: `QueryHistoryRow` gets the `workspace_id` column; `insert_query_history` reads `record.get("workspace_id")` onto the row; `list_query_history` takes a required `workspace_id: str` keyword and adds `.where(QueryHistoryRow.workspace_id == workspace_id)`, mirroring `list_saved_queries`'s existing pattern. The 200-row global eviction cap is deliberately left global (not made per-workspace) — an unrelated fairness/perf decision, out of scope for this confidentiality fix. `queries.py`: `track_query` takes a required `workspace_id: str` and stamps it on the inserted record; `get_query_history` takes a `Request`, derives `wsid = current_workspace_id(request)`, and passes it through to `list_query_history` — mirrors `list_saved_queries` (`queries.py:501-510`) exactly; `save_query_history` takes a `Request` and derives `wsid` server-side, **never** reading a client-supplied `workspace_id` from the payload — mirrors `create_saved_query` (`queries.py:513`), which does the same for the sibling table. `chat.py`: `_track_query` takes a required `workspace_id: str` and forwards it into `track_query`; its one call site (`chat_endpoint`, `POST /chat`) passes `current_workspace_id(http_request)` (`current_workspace_id` was already imported in `chat.py`, no new import needed).
- **Tests:** new `aurabackend/tests/test_query_history_tenant_isolation.py` (3/3 pass) — `test_list_query_history_scoped_to_caller_workspace` seeds one query-history record for `orgA` and one for `orgB` via `persistence.insert_query_history` directly, then asserts `queries.get_query_history` (called with a fake `Request` per org, same `_FakeRequest`/`state.user` pattern as `test_evolution.py`'s `TestEvolutionAPITenantIsolation`) returns only the caller's own record. `test_save_query_history_stamps_callers_workspace_not_client_payload` POSTs a payload with a spoofed `"workspace_id": "orgB"` while authenticated as `orgA` and asserts the persisted row is stamped `orgA`, not `orgB` — proves the server-derived `wsid` wins over the client body. `test_track_query_from_chat_stamps_caller_workspace` resolves `current_workspace_id` from a fake `orgA` request the way `chat_endpoint`'s call site does, feeds it into `chat.py`'s `_track_query`, and asserts the resulting `gateway_query_history` row is visible under `orgA` and absent under `orgB`. Signature-change fallout: `aurabackend/tests/test_api_gateway_persistence.py`'s existing `list_query_history`/`insert_query_history` contract tests were updated to pass/seed a `workspace_id` (previously untenanted calls) — full file re-run 50/50 pass alongside `test_tenant_isolation.py`. `test_chat_commander.py` + `test_chat_commander_pipeline_storage_backend.py` + `test_chat_pipeline.py` + `test_chat_resilience.py` (the files exercising `chat_endpoint`'s `POST /chat` path, which calls `_track_query`) re-run clean, 29/29 — required clearing the session's stale shared `GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL` SQLite file (`%TEMP%/aura_gateway_test.db`, `sqlite3.OperationalError: no such column: gateway_query_history.workspace_id` on first re-run) since SQLAlchemy's dev-fallback `create_all` doesn't alter existing tables — expected, not a code defect; a fresh CI run starts with no such file. All seven affected files re-run together, 82/82 pass. `alembic upgrade head` against a fresh DB applies the full ten-migration chain cleanly through the new `e5f6a7b8c9d0` head; `alembic heads` confirms a single head. Ruff (`ruff check --fix . --ignore E501,E402,F401,W191,W291,W293,F841,E701,E712,F823` repo-wide, and `ruff check --select E,F,I,W --ignore E501,E402,F401,E701,E712` on every touched file) — clean.
## BUG-041: the universal SSE stream bus has zero tenant scoping — any authenticated caller can subscribe to any topic, including the wildcards, and receive every other tenant's live query results, ETL/pipeline progress, and agent output
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** tenant-isolation-audit-followup workflow, 2026-09-03.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature — `aurabackend/shared/streaming_manager.py` (the in-process pub/sub bus every SSE stream is built on) fanned events out purely by topic-pattern match, with no concept of tenant at all. `GET /stream/{topic}` (`aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/stream.py`) is correctly gated by `JWTAuthMiddleware` when armed (a valid Bearer token is required), but the router never scoped the *subscription* itself past that point — any authenticated caller (of any tenant) could subscribe to any exact topic (`query:{other_tenant_job_id}`) if they could guess/observe the id, and the documented wildcards `/stream/*` and `/stream/monitor:*` made guessing unnecessary: a subscriber on `"*"` topic-matched (and, pre-fix, received) literally every event published by every tenant — live SQL query results, ETL/pipeline run progress, and agent execution output, all included. The same gap applied to the `Last-Event-ID`/`replay=true` buffered-replay path, which could hand back another tenant's already-published events on reconnect. This is the same tenant-isolation class already closed for webhooks (BUG-016), metadata_store (BUG-018), evolution (BUG-019), and synthetic jobs — auth-without-tenant-scoping recurring at a new chokepoint.
- **Root cause:** `StreamingManager._subscribers` stored only `(topic_pattern, queue)` per subscriber and `publish()`'s fanout condition was purely `self._matches(event.topic, pattern)` — no tenant dimension existed anywhere in the bus's data model, so there was nothing *to* check. `stream.py`'s `_event_generator`/`stream_topic` called `streaming_manager.subscribe(topic)` and `get_buffered_events(topic, ...)` with no caller identity at all, despite `current_workspace_id(request)` (the tenant-boundary helper already used by every sibling scoped router — `queries.py`, `pipelines.py`, `inbound_hooks.py`) sitting one import away.
- **Caused by:** none — the streaming bus was built to demonstrate live progress across the whole app (S20/STREAMING_FOUNDATIONS.md) before multi-tenant auth (`JWTAuthMiddleware`, `current_workspace_id`) existed in the codebase, and was never revisited once tenant scoping was added everywhere else.
- **Fix:** `aurabackend/shared/streaming_manager.py` — `StreamEvent` gained a `workspace_id: Optional[str] = None` field; `_subscribers` now stores `(pattern, workspace_id, queue)` 3-tuples; a new `StreamingManager._workspace_allowed(event_workspace_id, subscriber_workspace_id)` static helper returns `True` when either side is untenanted (global broadcast, or an unauthenticated dev-mode subscriber — the same fail-open-only-in-open-mode contract `current_workspace_id()` already has) or both match exactly, `False` otherwise. `publish()`'s fanout now requires `_workspace_allowed(...)` in addition to the existing topic-pattern match — this is what actually closes the wildcard leak: `/stream/*`/`/stream/monitor:*` still topic-match everything, but now only fan out events whose `workspace_id` equals the subscriber's own tenant (or is untenanted). `get_buffered_events()` gained the same `workspace_id` parameter and filters the buffered list identically, so Last-Event-ID replay and the `replay=true` bulk path can't leak buffered cross-tenant events either. `publish_progress`/`publish_complete`/`publish_error`/`broadcast` each gained an optional `workspace_id` kwarg forwarded straight into the `StreamEvent` they build.
  `aurabackend/api_gateway/routers/stream.py` — `_event_generator` and `stream_topic` both resolve `workspace_id = current_workspace_id(request)` and thread it into every `subscribe()`/`get_buffered_events()` call (including both `replay=true` call sites). No route signature or URL shape changed — `GET /stream/{topic}` behaves identically for a single tenant; only the server-side fanout/replay filter is new.
  Every tenant-scoped publisher call site now stamps `workspace_id` on its events, the tenant resolved eagerly in the request handler before any background-task closure (mirroring the existing `tenant = _request_tenant(request)` pattern in `pipelines.py::pipeline_execute_async`, since the background task outlives the request): `etl.py::etl_execute` (5 call sites, `TOPIC_ETL`), `files.py::upload_universal` (5 call sites, `TOPIC_UPLOAD`), `queries.py::execute_for_chat`'s sandbox-proxy branch (5 call sites, `TOPIC_QUERY`), `pipelines.py::pipeline_execute_async` and its nested `_kafka_cb` closure (`TOPIC_PIPELINE`), `agents/api.py::execute_prompt_async` (which did not previously take a `Request` at all — added one, the only router in the codebase missing it; `execute_prompt_stream` is untouched, it streams via its own local `progress_queue`, never touching the shared bus), and `inbound_hooks.py`'s three trigger paths: `fire_hook`'s own announce-event uses `hook.workspace_id` directly (already on the `InboundHook` model per BUG-016); `_fire_pipeline` reuses the `workspace_id` it already resolves via `get_pipeline_workspace_id()` (previously only used to derive `tenant`, now also threaded into its `publish`/`publish_error`/`publish_complete` calls); `_fire_agent` gained a new `workspace_id: Optional[str]` parameter, supplied from `fire_hook`'s `hook.workspace_id`. `shared/webhook_dispatcher.py`'s delivery-logging `publish(StreamEvent(topic="webhooks:deliveries", ...))` now passes `workspace_id=record.workspace_id` as an actual filter key — previously that tenant tag existed only inside the JSON payload body (cosmetic), so any tenant subscribed to `webhooks:deliveries` saw every other tenant's delivery records. `main.py`'s two `system:health` broadcasts are deliberately left untouched (`workspace_id=None`, genuinely global system data, exactly what `_workspace_allowed` treats as visible-to-everyone by design).
- **Tests:** new `aurabackend/tests/test_stream_tenant_isolation.py`, 10/10 pass. `StreamingManager`-level: two subscribers on the identical topic with different `workspace_id`s (only the matching one receives); the direct wildcard-leak regression (`"*"` and `"monitor:*"` subscribers for tenant B never receive tenant A's event — the exact scenario this bug describes, confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code and pass against the fix); an untenanted (`workspace_id=None`) event still reaches every subscriber regardless of tenant, and an untenanted subscriber still sees tenanted events (both prove the deliberate "leave global broadcasts alone" contract is preserved); `get_buffered_events` replay isolation across two tenants sharing one topic; an explicit `_workspace_allowed` truth-table test. Router-level, end-to-end through the real `stream_topic()` route coroutine and its production `StreamingResponse.body_iterator` (not a stub): a live-delivery test proves a connection authenticated as tenant A (via a real `shared.auth` JWT decoded exactly as `JWTAuthMiddleware` does) never receives a concurrently-published tenant-B event on the same topic but does receive its own tenant's event, and a replay test proves `replay=true` excludes another tenant's buffered event. Note on test construction: `stream.py`'s SSE connection is intentionally infinite (heartbeats until disconnect), which `httpx.ASGITransport` cannot drive concurrently with a mid-connection publish — it runs the whole ASGI app to completion before returning any bytes at all, confirmed by direct probe to hang identically even with no middleware involved — a testing-harness limitation (real deployments stream incrementally via uvicorn), not a product bug, so these two tests call the undecorated `stream_topic()` coroutine directly against a hand-built `Request` instead of going through `TestClient`/`ASGITransport`. Full re-run for regression: `test_middleware.py` + `test_webhook_dispatcher.py` + `test_collab.py` (75/75), plus every test file covering the touched publisher call sites — `test_etl_preview_source_traversal.py`, `test_etl_storage_backend.py`, `test_files_storage.py`, `test_files_upload_dataset_profile_bug030.py`, `test_inbound_hooks.py`, `test_pipeline_execution.py`, `test_pipeline_storage_backend.py`, `test_agents.py`, `test_agent_context_metadata.py` (80/80) — all clean, no regressions from the new `workspace_id` kwargs.

## BUG-042: ~35 async route handlers and background loops across the gateway routers, agent specialists, pipeline engine, and UASR call blocking synchronous code inline — freezes the single uvicorn worker for every tenant
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** `async-blocking-call-audit` workflow, 2026-09-04 (audit → 3-vote adversarial verify → implement → independent review, across 5 parallel scan groups covering the whole `aurabackend/` tree). The workflow's first two runs executed against a stale local `main` (6 PRs behind, missing BUG-036 through BUG-041) — a fork-based verification pass caught this before anything shipped, confirming the diff would have silently reverted BUG-039/040/041's tenant-scoping code in `queries.py` and `counterfactual_service/main.py`. The branch was rebased onto real `main` by hand (4 real conflicts resolved by keeping both the tenant-scoping logic and the blocking-call fixes) and independently re-verified from scratch before landing.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (availability) — `backend.md`'s "Async safety" rule is explicit: this deployment runs **one** uvicorn worker, so any blocking call inside an `async def` handler stalls the event loop and freezes every concurrent request for every tenant until it returns. This bundle closes ~35 such sites found across the gateway's HTTP routers, the agent-specialist LLM call sites, the pipeline engine/generator, and the UASR MAPE-K loop. Bundled as one bug (not ~35) because every finding is the identical root cause and the identical fix — this mirrors BUG-035's precedent (5 files bundled as one bug for the same reason); splitting these into 35 separate PRs would be pure process overhead with no lineage-tracking benefit, since none of them can regress independently of the shared pattern.
- **Root cause:** each site called a synchronous, potentially-slow operation (LLM SDK calls via `llm.generate`/`generate_json`, `bcrypt` password hashing/verification, DuckDB load/transform/COPY, pandas `to_parquet`, blocking file/dir I/O, `WebhookDispatcher`'s disk-writing `.delete()`/`.create()`, and the UASR drift-detector's `.detect()`) directly inside an `async def` function or a tight `while True` background loop, with no `asyncio.to_thread` offload — the same class BUG-021/022 partially closed earlier, but not exhaustively.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing gaps of the same known class as BUG-021/022, not a regression from any recent change.
- **Fix:** every site wraps its blocking call in `await asyncio.to_thread(...)` (or, where several blocking statements form one sequential unit — e.g. `etl.py::etl_execute`'s load→transform→COPY sequence, `connections.py::sync_connection_table`'s DataFrame→parquet serialization — the whole unit is wrapped as one closure, mirroring the existing `dashboards.py::_run_tile` pattern). No behavior or return-value changes anywhere; this is purely an execution-context change.
  - `api_gateway/routers/auth.py`: `_issue_token_password`'s `verify_password` (bcrypt) and `register_user`'s `hash_password` (bcrypt) both offloaded.
  - `api_gateway/routers/connections.py::sync_connection_table`: the pandas `DataFrame`→parquet serialization offloaded into `_to_parquet_bytes()`.
  - `api_gateway/routers/etl.py::etl_execute`: the whole load→transform→COPY sequence offloaded via a `_run_pipeline()` closure over an outer `con = new_connection()` (mirrors `etl_preview_source`'s existing pattern) — `workspace_id`-tagged `publish_progress` calls (BUG-041) preserved on both sides of the merge.
  - `api_gateway/routers/pipelines.py`, `pipeline/engine.py`, `pipeline/generator.py`: `PipelineGenerator.get_file_schema`/`.generate`/`.suggest_steps` and `PipelineEngine`'s DuckDB execution paths offloaded.
  - `api_gateway/routers/queries.py::get_dashboard_stats`: the tenant upload-dir `readdir`+per-entry-`stat` scan extracted to `_list_tracked_files()` and offloaded.
  - `api_gateway/routers/webhooks.py`: `delete_webhook`'s `WebhookDispatcher.delete()` (disk write) offloaded, matching the existing `create_webhook`/`update_webhook` offload pattern.
  - `agents/planner.py` + all 9 `agents/specialists/*.py` files (`analysis`, `counterfactual_parser`, `dar_research`, `intent`, `optimization`, `pipeline`, `sql_generator`, `transform`, `visualization`): every specialist's synchronous LLM call (`llm.generate`/`generate_json`) offloaded — `intent_agent.py` and `sql_generator_agent.py` are the highest-traffic call sites in the app (run on every chat message / every SQL generation respectively).
  - `evolution/engine.py::EvolutionEngine._generate_improvement_proposal`: `llm.generate_json` offloaded.
  - `uasr/actuator_agent.py`: `_llm_shim`'s `llm.generate` offloaded. `uasr/reflector_agent.py`: `_llm_diagnosis`'s `llm.generate_json` offloaded. `uasr/mapek_worker.py::_run_forever`: `_analyze_detect_drift(batch)` (can hit a blocking Redis round-trip under the Redis state backend) offloaded — the same `detect()` call `service.py`'s HTTP handlers already wrap.
- **Tests:** 137/137 pass across the full PR1 test set — `test_agents.py`, `test_analysis_agent.py`, `test_auth.py`, `test_chat_pipeline.py`, `test_connections_sync.py`, `test_dar_research_agent.py`, `test_etl_preview_source_traversal.py`, `test_etl_storage_backend.py`, `test_intent_agent.py`, `test_pipeline_agent.py`, `test_pipeline_execution.py`, `test_pipeline_generator.py`, `test_pipeline_storage_backend.py`, `test_reflector_agent_llm_diagnosis.py`, `test_scalability_fixes.py`, `test_sql_generator_explain.py`, `test_transform_agent.py`, `test_uasr_actuator_shims.py`, `test_webhooks_router_bug_async_save.py`. Every new/extended test proves the offload directly — either a `monkeypatch` spy on `asyncio.to_thread` asserting the blocking function was dispatched through it, or a ticker-race pattern (a fast ticker coroutine races a deliberately slowed version of the blocking call; if the call ran inline the ticker would never get a chance to advance). Tenant-isolation regression sweep re-run clean: `test_tenant_isolation.py`, `test_query_history_tenant_isolation.py`, `test_stream_tenant_isolation.py`, `test_inbound_hooks.py`, `test_middleware.py`, `test_api_gateway_persistence.py` — 116/116 pass, confirming BUG-039/040/041's tenant-scoping logic survived the merge fully intact. Ruff (`--select E,F,I,W --ignore E501,E402,F401,E701,E712`) clean on every touched production file.

## BUG-043: 8 async route handlers in the counterfactual/financial-audit service call blocking synchronous code inline — freezes the single uvicorn worker for every tenant
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** `async-blocking-call-audit` workflow, 2026-09-04 — same audit pass as BUG-042, split into its own bug/PR because `counterfactual_service` is a distinct trust-sensitive subsystem (signed WORM audit artifacts) that already got its own dedicated tenant-isolation bug (BUG-040) earlier in this session, and mixing it into BUG-042's already-large gateway/agents/uasr bundle would have made the diff harder to review for exactly the class of regression (a silently-dropped `tenant_id` check) this session already caught once by chance.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (availability) — same root cause as BUG-042: this deployment runs a single uvicorn worker, so a blocking call inside an `async def` handler freezes every tenant's requests for its duration. `counterfactual_service/main.py` and `audit_worker.py` had 8 such sites: pandas file reads (`_resolve_dataset`), artifact reads/writes (`sign_and_persist`, `verify_report`, `pending_exceptions`, the bulk-replay endpoint), and `exception_queue.record_decision` (the same function BUG-040 added a tenant check to).
- **Root cause:** each site called synchronous pandas/file-I/O or the `exception_queue` module's synchronous artifact-store functions directly inside an `async def` handler, with no `asyncio.to_thread` offload — the same known class as BUG-021/022/042, not exhaustively closed until now.
- **Caused by:** none — pre-existing gaps of the same known class, not a regression from any recent change.
- **Fix:** every site wraps its blocking call in `await asyncio.to_thread(...)`, mirroring BUG-042's pattern exactly. `financial_audit_decide`'s `record_decision` call — the one BUG-040 modified to pass `tenant_id=_ledger_tenant(user)` — keeps that argument unchanged; the offload only changes *how* the call executes (in a worker thread), never *what* is passed to it. Verified directly: `tenant_id=_ledger_tenant(user)` still appears at both call sites in `main.py` (`financial_audit_decide` and the sibling ledger-append call).
- **Tests:** 50/50 pass across `test_audit_endpoint.py`, `test_counterfactual_engine.py`, `test_counterfactual_sprint13.py`, `test_exception_queue.py`, `test_financial_audit.py` — including BUG-040's own `test_record_decision_rejects_cross_tenant_auditor` and `test_exception_decide_rejects_cross_tenant_auditor_e2e`, confirmed still passing (the offload didn't disturb the cross-tenant rejection). This branch was built on a stale main (pre-BUG-042) initially — one test (`test_parser_extracts_treatment_outcome`, which exercises BUG-042's `counterfactual_parser_agent.py` offload) failed until rebasing onto post-BUG-042 main, at which point it passed as expected; not a defect in this bug's own fix. Ruff (`--select E,F,I,W --ignore E501,E402,F401,E701,E712`) clean on both touched files.

## BUG-044: `alembic/env.py` always targets `metadata_store.db.DATABASE_URL` regardless of which model's migration is running — every migration touching `api_gateway/persistence.py`'s or `shared/audit_ledger.py`'s tables silently applies to the wrong database on any deployment where those are separate SQLite files from the metadata store
- **Status:** fixed
- **Found by:** live investigation of BUG-039's supposedly-already-merged fix (query-history `workspace_id` column, PR #312), 2026-09-08, after `alembic current` on the box reported `e5f6a7b8c9d0 (head)` yet `PRAGMA table_info(gateway_query_history)` showed no `workspace_id` column at all. Re-running the migration (`alembic downgrade d4e5f6a7b8c9` then `alembic upgrade head`) reported success both times with no error, but `/data/state/gateway.db`'s mtime never changed — proving alembic wasn't writing to that file at all. Checking `/data/state/metadata.db` instead found a full, independent copy of every `gateway_*` table (`gateway_query_history`, `gateway_saved_queries`, `gateway_dashboards`, etc.) — and *that* copy's `gateway_query_history` correctly had `workspace_id` after the downgrade/upgrade. Alembic had been silently migrating a shadow copy of the gateway schema inside the metadata database this whole time.
- **Severity:** blocks-feature (silent) — on any deployment where `GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL` and `METADATA_DATABASE_URL` point at different databases (this box: `sqlite+aiosqlite:////data/state/gateway.db` vs `sqlite+aiosqlite:////data/state/metadata.db`, per `docker-compose.yml`'s SQLite-fallback comment), **every Alembic migration that touches a `gateway_*` or `audit_ledger` table has been silently applying to the metadata database instead of the one the live app actually reads and writes.** `alembic current`/`alembic heads` report success and a clean single head, giving zero indication anything is wrong — the only symptom is the application code hitting `sqlite3.OperationalError: no such column: ...` (or, for BUG-039 specifically, the tenant-scoping fix silently not taking effect: `list_query_history`'s `.where(QueryHistoryRow.workspace_id == workspace_id)` filter against a column that doesn't exist on the table the app actually queries). This has presumably been true since the first migration that touched a `gateway_*` table (S20-era) — the box's `gateway.db` has been running on an ORM `create_all()`-only schema (confirmed: `gateway.db` has no `alembic_version` table at all) while every migration file in `aurabackend/alembic/versions/` that names a gateway table has actually been applied only to `metadata.db`'s shadow copy.
